与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 阻断抗体水平相关的信号开启 DNA 邻近测定

A Signal Turn-On DNA Proximity Assay That Correlates with SARS-CoV-2 Blocking Antibody Levels
作者： Yan Shan Ang, Lin-Yue Lanry Yung
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在通过开发一种与阻断抗体水平而不是 RBD 结合相关的新测定法来解决 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 中和抗体现有替代测定法的局限性。
设计与对象
这项概念验证研究引入了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的信号开启双抗原测定,针对具有已知 RBD 结合和 ACE2 阻断特性的三种商业抗体测试了三种特征明确的变体(2019、Delta 和 Omicron)。
核心方法
DNA 邻近测定 (DPA) 旨在测量 RBD 寡核苷酸起始探针在目标抗体桥接后的邻近激活,从而能够通过荧光信号生成的动力学速率进行定量。
主要结果
DPA 结果与抗体阻断 ACE2 的能力相关,而不是与抗体与 RBD 的结合相关,并且该测定显示出良好的分析性能,具有 ng/mL 检测限和高精度(<5% 测定间变异系数)。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为证明了 DPA 平台的新颖性,与传统的终点测量相比,该平台为量化封闭抗体提供了一种更稳健、更灵敏的方法。
科研与公卫意义
The findings suggest that the DPA platform could improve the surveillance and management of SARS-CoV-2 neutralizing antibodies, offering a scalable and reliable tool for post-COVID-19 and future outbreak scenarios.
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于摘要分析,其证据基于一个概念验证设计,仅测试了三种变体和三种商业抗体,可能无法充分代表严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的多样性或临床人群。

A Signal Turn-On DNA Proximity Assay That Correlates with SARS-CoV-2 Blocking Antibody Levels

Authors: Yan Shan Ang, Lin-Yue Lanry Yung
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Surrogate in vitro assays have enabled the scalable testing of neutralizing antibodies to SARS-CoV-2 as a toolbox to manage the post-COVID-19 situation and future outbreaks. However, most surrogate assays use a competitive format to mimic the blocking event between the receptor binding domain (RBD) and the human angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (ACE2) receptor. This signal “turn-off” format has a narrow dynamic range and is semiquantitative at best. In this proof-of-concept work, we introduce a signal “turn-on” dual antigen assay based on the proximity activation of a pair of RBD-oligonucleotide initiator probes upon bridging by the target antibody. Three well-characterized SARS-CoV-2 variants, i.e., 2019 (wild-type), Delta (B.1.617.2), and Omicron (B.1.1.529), were tested against three commercial antibodies with known RBD-binding and ACE2-blocking profiles. The DNA proximity assay (DPA) results correlated with the ability of the antibodies to block ACE2 instead of their binding to the RBD. Moreover, the single-step, wash-free property of DPA enabled quantification using the kinetic rate of fluorescence signal generation which was more robust than conventional end-point measurements. The DPA achieved good analytical performance with ng/mL detection limit and high assay precision (<5% interassay coefficient of variation), as well as good spike-and-recovery and dilution linearity in serum matrix. The test outcome from three sets of validated vaccine time-series samples were concordant with reference values, demonstrating the potential clinical utility of the DPA platform.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the limitations of existing surrogate assays for SARS-CoV-2 neutralizing antibodies by developing a novel assay that correlates with blocking antibody levels rather than RBD binding.
Design and population
This proof-of-concept study introduced a signal turn-on dual antigen assay for SARS-CoV-2, testing three well-characterized variants (2019, Delta, and Omicron) against three commercial antibodies with known RBD-binding and ACE2-blocking profiles.
Core methods
The DNA proximity assay (DPA) was designed to measure the proximity activation of RBD-oligonucleotide initiator probes upon bridging by the target antibody, enabling quantification through the kinetic rate of fluorescence signal generation.
Main results
The DPA results correlated with the ability of the antibodies to block ACE2 rather than their binding to the RBD, and the assay demonstrated good analytical performance with ng/mL detection limit and high precision (<5% interassay coefficient of variation).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as demonstrating the novelty of the DPA platform, which provides a more robust and sensitive method for quantifying blocking antibodies compared to conventional end-point measurements.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that the DPA platform could improve the surveillance and management of SARS-CoV-2 neutralizing antibodies, offering a scalable and reliable tool for post-COVID-19 and future outbreak scenarios.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to an abstract-only analysis, and the evidence is based on a proof-of-concept design with three variants and three commercial antibodies, which may not fully represent the breadth of SARS-CoV-2 diversity or clinical populations.

多维免疫特征预测接种疫苗的个体对 Omicron 感染的易感性

A Multidimensional Immune Signature Predicts Susceptibility to Omicron Infection in Vaccinated Individuals
作者： Hend Jarras, Bazie WW, Isalie Blais, Benjamin Goyer, Julien Boucher, Arielle Pakenham, Kimberly Dancause-Caron, Henintsoa Rabezanahary, Theriault, M., Kim Santerre
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
尽管 疫苗接种 广泛存在,但个体间对病毒感染的易感性仍然存在显着差异,并且其免疫学基础仍然知之甚少。
设计与对象
我们比较了两组接种疫苗的个体,这些个体在第一波 Omicron 波期间要么未感染,要么被感染。
核心方法
在基线和 24 周后采集血样。分离外周血单核细胞 (PBMC) 和多形核中性粒细胞 (PMN),并用 TLR7/8 激动剂 R848 刺激,以评估先天反应。用 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 肽库和高度纯化的灭活病毒(ancestral 和 Omicron BA.1)刺激 PBMC,以评估适应性免疫。
主要结果
在感染之前,尽管免疫表型基本相似,但感染组中的个体表现出 CD4 和 CD8 T 细胞增殖反应减少,同时在所有刺激条件下 TNF 产量增加,这表明预先存在功能性免疫缺陷。
解释与创新
这种功能缺陷伴随着炎症活动的加剧,包括TNF和IFN-γ产生增加、抗核衣壳IgG3水平升高、B细胞和骨髓细胞频率升高、循环中干扰素诱导性T细胞α趋化剂(I-TAC)浓度降低以及PMN IL-8反应适度受损。
科研与公卫意义
总之,这些结果表明,对 Omicron 感染的易感性与预先存在且持续的功能性免疫失衡有关,影响先天性和适应性免疫。
局限与证据强度
所提供的证据并未报告局限性。

A Multidimensional Immune Signature Predicts Susceptibility to Omicron Infection in Vaccinated Individuals

Authors: Hend Jarras, Bazie WW, Isalie Blais, Benjamin Goyer, Julien Boucher, Arielle Pakenham, Kimberly Dancause-Caron, Henintsoa Rabezanahary, Theriault, M., Kim Santerre
Original abstract (English)
Substantial inter-individual variation in susceptibility to viral infection persists despite widespread vaccination, and its immunological basis remains poorly understood. We investigated how innate and adaptive immune responses contribute to susceptibility to SARS-CoV-2 infection during the early COVID-19 pandemic. We compared two groups of vaccinated individuals who either remained uninfected or became infected during the first Omicron wave. Blood samples were collected at baseline and 24 weeks later. Peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) and polymorphonuclear neutrophils (PMNs) were isolated and stimulated with the TLR7/8 agonist R848 to assess innate responses. PBMCs were stimulated with SARS-CoV-2 peptide pools and highly purified inactivated viruses (ancestral and Omicron BA.1) to evaluate adaptive immunity. Prior to infection, individuals in the infected group exhibited reduced CD4 and CD8 T cells proliferative responses, alongside with increased TNF production across all stimulation conditions, despite largely comparable immune phenotypes, indicating a pre-existing functional immune deficit. Following infection, T-cell proliferation and IFN-gamma production were partially restored in response to viral antigens, although responses to Omicron BA.1 remained suboptimal. This functional deficit was accompanied by heightened inflammatory activity, including increased TNF and IFN-gamma production, elevated anti-nucleocapsid IgG3 levels, higher frequencies of B cells and myeloid cells, reduced circulating interferon-inducible T-cell Alpha Chemoattractant (I-TAC) concentrations, and a modest impairment in PMN IL-8 responses. Notably, these alterations were detectable prior to infection and persisted thereafter, indicating that they represent determinants rather than consequences of viral infection. Importantly, beyond differences in the magnitude of immune responses, protection was associated with the degree of functional coordination within the humoral compartment, as reflected by the relationship between Spike-binding antibodies and neutralizing activity. Together, these results demonstrate that susceptibility to Omicron infection is linked to a pre-existing and persistent functional immune imbalance affecting both innate and adaptive arms of immunity.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Substantial inter-individual variation in susceptibility to viral infection persists despite widespread vaccination, and its immunological basis remains poorly understood.
Design and population
We compared two groups of vaccinated individuals who either remained uninfected or became infected during the first Omicron wave.
Core methods
Blood samples were collected at baseline and 24 weeks later. Peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) and polymorphonuclear neutrophils (PMNs) were isolated and stimulated with the TLR7/8 agonist R848 to assess innate responses. PBMCs were stimulated with SARS-CoV-2 peptide pools and highly purified inactivated viruses (ancestral and Omicron BA.1) to evaluate adaptive immunity.
Main results
Prior to infection, individuals in the infected group exhibited reduced CD4 and CD8 T cells proliferative responses, alongside with increased TNF production across all stimulation conditions, despite largely comparable immune phenotypes, indicating a pre-existing functional immune deficit.
Interpretation and novelty
This functional deficit was accompanied by heightened inflammatory activity, including increased TNF and IFN-gamma production, elevated anti-nucleocapsid IgG3 levels, higher frequencies of B cells and myeloid cells, reduced circulating interferon-inducible T-cell Alpha Chemoattractant (I-TAC) concentrations, and a modest impairment in PMN IL-8 responses.
Scientific and public-health significance
Together, these results demonstrate that susceptibility to Omicron infection is linked to a pre-existing and persistent functional immune imbalance affecting both innate and adaptive arms of immunity.
Limitations and evidence strength
The supplied evidence does not report limitations.

基于 Nsp8 的蛋白质抑制剂的结构启发设计可抑制 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制

Structure-inspired design of Nsp8-based protein inhibitors to suppress SARS-CoV-2 replication
作者： Petra Štěrbová, Hsin-Hung Lin, I-Ping Tu, Wei-Hau Chang
摘要中文翻译摘要 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 依赖于由 nsp12 及其辅因子 nsp7 和 nsp8 组成的保守 RNA 依赖性 RNA 聚合酶 (RdRp) 复合物来复制其 RNA 基因组。虽然大多数 抗病毒药物 策略直接针对病毒酶或表面蛋白,但另一种方法是使用源自病原体本身的缺陷成分来破坏基本病毒分子机器的组装或功能。在这里,以 nsp12-nsp7-nsp8 复制复合物的结构分析为指导,我们设计了截短的 nsp8 蛋白,该蛋白保留了与 nsp12 结合的能力,但在接合 RNA 方面存在缺陷。使用能够进行 RNA 引物延伸的纯化 nsp12–nsp7–nsp8 系统,我们表明,选定的截短 nsp8 变体在引入其他功能复合物时会抑制聚合酶活性。这些结果与竞争机制一致,其中缺陷的 nsp8 变体与 nsp12 结合并干扰野生型 nsp8 的掺入或功能,从而损害生产性复制复合物的形成。为了进一步探索这一策略,我们使用 nsp8-nsp12 界面的结构引导计算机分析来识别相互作用热点并筛选相应的单氨基酸取代。几种变体在重构聚合酶测定中表现出增强的抑制活性。总之,这些发现建立了一种概念验证策略,其中结构工程化的病原体衍生蛋白可以充当重要病毒复制机制的显性失活抑制剂。这种方法为开发基于蛋白质或肽的抑制剂提供了一个框架,这些抑制剂针对病毒复制复合物内保守的蛋白质-蛋白质相互作用。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在通过针对必需的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制复合物开发一种新的 抗病毒药物 策略,该复制复合物对于 RNA 基因组 复制至关重要。这种方法满足了对直接酶或表面蛋白抑制之外的替代治疗靶点的需求。
设计与对象
该研究涉及能够进行 RNA 引物延伸的纯化 nsp12–nsp7–nsp8 系统,该系统用于测试截短的 nsp8 变体的抑制效果。研究重点是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 及其复制机制。
核心方法
研究人员根据 nsp12-nsp7-nsp8 复合物的结构分析设计了截短的 nsp8 蛋白。他们使用计算机分析来识别相互作用热点并筛选单氨基酸取代以增强抑制活性。采用重构聚合酶测定来评估这些变体的效果。
主要结果
当引入功能性 nsp12-nsp7-nsp8 复合物时,选定的截短 nsp8 变体显示出抑制聚合酶活性。几种变体在聚合酶测定中表现出增强的抑制活性。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,结构工程化的病原体衍生蛋白可以作为重要病毒复制机制的显性失活抑制剂,为 抗病毒药物 的开发提供新策略。
科研与公卫意义
这种方法为开发针对病毒复制复合体中保守的蛋白质–蛋白质相互作用的基于蛋白质或肽的抑制剂提供了框架,这可能为未来研究严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2和相关病毒的抗病毒药物疗法提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于使用纯化系统的体外实验,可能无法完全代表体内条件。证据是中等的,因为它基于仅抽象数据,不包括详细的临床或流行病学结果。

Structure-inspired design of Nsp8-based protein inhibitors to suppress SARS-CoV-2 replication

Authors: Petra Štěrbová, Hsin-Hung Lin, I-Ping Tu, Wei-Hau Chang
Original abstract (English)
Abstract SARS-CoV-2 relies on a conserved RNA-dependent RNA polymerase (RdRp) complex composed of nsp12 and its cofactors nsp7 and nsp8 to replicate its RNA genome. Whereas most antiviral strategies target viral enzymes or surface proteins directly, an alternative approach is to disrupt the assembly or function of an essential viral molecular machine using a defective component derived from the pathogen itself. Here, guided by structural analyses of the nsp12–nsp7–nsp8 replication complex, we designed truncated nsp8 proteins that retain the ability to associate with nsp12 but are defective in engaging RNA. Using a purified nsp12–nsp7–nsp8 system capable of RNA primer extension, we show that selected truncated nsp8 variants inhibit polymerase activity when introduced into an otherwise functional complex. These results are consistent with a competitive mechanism in which the defective nsp8 variants associate with nsp12 and interfere with incorporation or function of wild-type nsp8, thereby compromising formation of a productive replication complex. To further explore this strategy, we used structure-guided in silico analysis of the nsp8– nsp12 interface to identify interaction hotspots and screened corresponding single-amino-acid substitutions. Several variants exhibited enhanced inhibitory activity in the reconstituted polymerase assay. Together, these findings establish a proof-of-concept strategy in which a structurally engineered, pathogen-derived protein can act as a dominant-negative inhibitor of an essential viral replication machinery. This approach provides a framework for developing protein- or peptide-based inhibitors that target conserved protein–protein interactions within viral replication complexes.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to develop a novel antiviral strategy by targeting the essential SARS-CoV-2 replication complex, which is critical for RNA genome replication. This approach addresses the need for alternative therapeutic targets beyond direct enzyme or surface protein inhibition.
Design and population
The study involved a purified nsp12–nsp7–nsp8 system capable of RNA primer extension, which was used to test the inhibitory effects of truncated nsp8 variants. The research focused on SARS-CoV-2 and its replication machinery.
Core methods
The researchers designed truncated nsp8 proteins based on structural analyses of the nsp12–nsp7–nsp8 complex. They used in silico analysis to identify interaction hotspots and screened single-amino-acid substitutions to enhance inhibitory activity. A reconstituted polymerase assay was employed to evaluate the effects of these variants.
Main results
Selected truncated nsp8 variants were shown to inhibit polymerase activity when introduced into a functional nsp12–nsp7–nsp8 complex. Several variants exhibited enhanced inhibitory activity in the polymerase assay.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that structurally engineered, pathogen-derived proteins can act as dominant-negative inhibitors of essential viral replication machinery, offering a new strategy for antiviral development.
Scientific and public-health significance
This approach provides a framework for developing protein- or peptide-based inhibitors targeting conserved protein–protein interactions within viral replication complexes, which could inform future research on antiviral therapies for SARS-CoV-2 and related viruses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro experiments using a purified system, which may not fully represent in vivo conditions. The evidence is moderate as it is based on abstract-only data and does not include detailed clinical or epidemiological outcomes.

Omicron BA.5 波期间 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染住院患者的血培养阳性、微生物谱和炎症相关性:一项回顾性 队列研究 研究

Blood Culture Positivity, Microbiological Spectrum, and Inflammatory Correlates in Patients Hospitalized with SARS-CoV-2 Infection During the Omicron BA.5 Wave: A Retrospective Cohort Study
作者： Cristiana Georgeta Bujor, Marilena Dinuti, Felicia Sfrijan, Dinuti
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to characterize blood-culture positivity, pathogen distribution, inflammatory correlates, antimicrobial exposure, and short-term outcomes in a homogeneous cohort hospitalized during the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron BA.5 wave. Blood cultures are frequently obtained in hospitalized patients with COVID-19, although the diagnostic yield and clinical meaning of culture positivity vary markedly according to case mix, disease severity, and timing of sampling.
设计与对象
这项回顾性观察研究包括 2022 年 7 月 12 日至 9 月 25 日期间因 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染住院的 395 名患者。当记录到指定细菌种类时,患者被归类为血培养阳性,当记录到“无生长”时,患者被归类为培养阴性。Omicron 时代的数据仍然相对有限。
核心方法
连续变量使用 Mann-Whitney U 检验进行比较,分类变量使用 Fisher 精确检验或卡方检验(视情况而定)进行比较。使用简约的多变量逻辑回归模型来探索与血培养阳性独立相关的因素。
主要结果
Blood cultures were positive in 89/395 patients (22.5%). Gram-positive organisms accounted for 52.8% of positive cultures and Gram-negative organisms for 47.2%. In the adjusted model, higher leukocyte count remained independently associated with culture positivity (aOR 1.10 per 1 × 109/L, 95% CI 1.05–1.15; p < 0.001), while vaccination status showed an inverse exploratory association (aOR 0.58, 95% CI 0.35–0.96; p = 0.033).
解释与创新
血培养阳性在该住院 BA.5 队列研究 中很常见,并且显示出几乎平衡的革兰氏阳性/革兰氏阴性分布。白细胞增多和临床记录的败血症是阳性最明显的相关性,而 死亡率 没有显着差异。
科研与公卫意义
These findings support selective blood-culture use driven by bacterial infection signals and emphasize the need to connect antimicrobial decisions to microbiological evidence. A documented sepsis diagnosis was more frequent in the culture-positive group (48.3% vs. 28.1%; OR 2.39, 95% CI 1.47–3.88; p < 0.001).
局限与证据强度
探索性模型 AUC 为 0.672(bootstrap 95% CI 0.608–0.731);未评估校准。死亡率 的培养阳性率在数值上较高(20.2% vs. 14.4%),但未达到统计学显着性(p = 0.188)。

Blood Culture Positivity, Microbiological Spectrum, and Inflammatory Correlates in Patients Hospitalized with SARS-CoV-2 Infection During the Omicron BA.5 Wave: A Retrospective Cohort Study

Authors: Cristiana Georgeta Bujor, Marilena Dinuti, Felicia Sfrijan, Dinuti
Original abstract (English)
Blood cultures are frequently obtained in hospitalized patients with COVID-19, although the diagnostic yield and clinical meaning of culture positivity vary markedly according to case mix, disease severity, and timing of sampling. Omicron-era data remain comparatively limited. We aimed to characterize blood-culture positivity, pathogen distribution, inflammatory correlates, antimicrobial exposure, and short-term outcomes in a homogeneous cohort hospitalized during the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron BA.5 wave. This retrospective observational study included 395 patients hospitalized with SARS-CoV-2 infection between 12 July and 25 September 2022. Patients were categorized as blood-culture positive when a named bacterial species was recorded and as culture negative when “No growth” was documented. Continuous variables were compared using the Mann–Whitney U test and categorical variables using Fisher’s exact or chi-square tests, as appropriate. A parsimonious multivariable logistic regression model was used to explore factors independently associated with blood-culture positivity. Blood cultures were positive in 89/395 patients (22.5%). Gram-positive organisms accounted for 52.8% of positive cultures and Gram-negative organisms for 47.2%. The most frequent isolates were Streptococcus pneumoniae (20.2% of positive cultures), Enterococcus faecalis (18.0%), Klebsiella pneumoniae (18.0%), Staphylococcus aureus (14.6%), Escherichia coli (14.6%), and Pseudomonas aeruginosa (14.6%). Compared with patients with no growth, those with positive cultures had higher admission leukocyte counts (median 13.42 vs. 11.22 × 109/L; p < 0.001), C-reactive protein (116.15 vs. 105.50; p = 0.007), and interleukin-6 (17.24 vs. A documented sepsis diagnosis was more frequent in the culture-positive group (48.3% vs. 28.1%; OR 2.39, 95% CI 1.47–3.88; p < 0.001). Mortality was numerically higher with culture positivity (20.2% vs. 14.4%) but did not reach statistical significance (p = 0.188). In the adjusted model, higher leukocyte count remained independently associated with culture positivity (aOR 1.10 per 1 × 109/L, 95% CI 1.05–1.15; p < 0.001), while vaccination status showed an inverse exploratory association (aOR 0.58, 95% CI 0.35–0.96; p = 0.033). The exploratory model AUC was 0.672 (bootstrap 95% CI 0.608–0.731); calibration was not assessed. Blood-culture positivity was common in this hospitalized BA.5 cohort and showed a nearly balanced Gram-positive/Gram-negative distribution. Leukocytosis and clinically documented sepsis were the clearest correlates of positivity, whereas mortality did not differ significantly. These findings support selective blood-culture use driven by bacterial infection signals and emphasize the need to connect antimicrobial decisions to microbiological evidence.
View research seven-element analysis

The platform trial in COVID-19 priming and BOOsting (PICOBOO): The reactogenicity of nine licensed COVID-19 vaccines delivered in immunocompetent adults.

The platform trial in COVID-19 priming and BOOsting (PICOBOO): The reactogenicity of nine licensed COVID-19 vaccines delivered in immunocompetent adults.
作者： C M, Dymock M, K.L. Flanagan, M. Plebanski, H. Marshall, M.J. Estcourt, M.C. Tjiam, C.C. Blyth, K. Subbarao, S. Nicholson
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study aimed to evaluate the reactogenicity of nine licensed COVID-19 vaccines in immunocompetent adults, addressing the need to understand vaccine safety profiles during booster and additional dose administration.
设计与对象
PICOBOO is a randomised, Bayesian adaptive trial evaluating immunogenicity and reactogenicity of licensed COVID-19 vaccines in immunocompetent individuals aged >12 years. Participants were randomised within each stratum to receive a single vaccine dose. A total of 1049 COVID-19 vaccine doses were administered to 586 adults enrolled between March 2022 and September 2024.
核心方法
Individuals who had not received a COVID-19 vaccine within three months were eligible. Reactogenicity events were recorded on days 1-7 following vaccination. Serious or medically attended adverse events (AEs), AEs of special interest, and AEs resulting in withdrawal were collected through day 28. Participants were followed until day 720.
主要结果
786 次(75%)剂量后报告至少一种局部反应,798 次(76%)剂量后报告至少一种全身反应。局部反应原性范围为 NVX-CoV2373 (Nvx) 的 47% (110/236) 至 Moderna 的 90% (131/145),而全身反应原性范围为 Nvx 的 69% (162/236) 至 BNT162b2 的 85% (121/143)。严重的反应原性事件很少见。
解释与创新
反应原性事件很常见,但很少严重。尽管 Nvx 后的反应原性似乎比层内 mRNA 疫苗 出现的频率要低,但该试验并没有为 疫苗 之间的比较提供动力,并且应该对结果进行描述性解释。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为各种获得许可的 疫苗 的反应原性概况提供了描述性见解,这可以为未来免疫功能正常的成人的 疫苗 开发和给药策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
在第 1-7 天记录反应原性事件,并在第 28 天收集严重不良事件,这可能会限制长期影响的评估。

The platform trial in COVID-19 priming and BOOsting (PICOBOO): The reactogenicity of nine licensed COVID-19 vaccines delivered in immunocompetent adults.

Authors: C M, Dymock M, K.L. Flanagan, M. Plebanski, H. Marshall, M.J. Estcourt, M.C. Tjiam, C.C. Blyth, K. Subbarao, S. Nicholson
Original abstract (English)
PICOBOO is a randomised, Bayesian adaptive trial evaluating immunogenicity and reactogenicity of licensed COVID-19 vaccines in immunocompetent individuals aged >12 years. We present reactogenicity data for nine vaccines administered as additional doses within adult strata based on age cohort and primary vaccination regimen. Individuals who had not received a COVID-19 vaccine within three months were eligible. Participants were randomised within each stratum to receive a single vaccine dose. Reactogenicity events were recorded on days 1-7 following vaccination. Serious or medically attended adverse events (AEs), AEs of special interest, and AEs resulting in withdrawal were collected through day 28. Participants were followed until day 720. A total of 1049 COVID-19 vaccine doses were administered to 586 adults enrolled between March 2022 and September 2024. Of these, 335 (57%), 96 (16%), 97 (17%) and 58 (10%) were randomised one, two, three and four times, respectively. At least one local reaction was reported after 786 (75%) doses and at least one systemic reaction after 798 (76%) doses. Local reactogenicity ranged from 47% (110/236) with NVX-CoV2373 (Nvx) to 90% (131/145) with Moderna, while systemic reactogenicity ranged from 69% (162/236) with Nvx to 85% (121/143) with BNT162b2. Pain was the most common local reaction; headache, fatigue, myalgia, and chills were the most common systemic reactions. Severe reactogenicity events were rare. Reactogenicity events were common but rarely severe. Although reactogenicity appeared less frequent following Nvx than mRNA vaccines within strata, the trial was not powered for between-vaccine comparisons and findings should be interpreted descriptively. Medical Research Future Fund (2014690/2016473) and The Snow Foundation.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to evaluate the reactogenicity of nine licensed COVID-19 vaccines in immunocompetent adults, addressing the need to understand vaccine safety profiles during booster and additional dose administration.
Design and population
PICOBOO is a randomised, Bayesian adaptive trial evaluating immunogenicity and reactogenicity of licensed COVID-19 vaccines in immunocompetent individuals aged >12 years. Participants were randomised within each stratum to receive a single vaccine dose. A total of 1049 COVID-19 vaccine doses were administered to 586 adults enrolled between March 2022 and September 2024.
Core methods
Individuals who had not received a COVID-19 vaccine within three months were eligible. Reactogenicity events were recorded on days 1-7 following vaccination. Serious or medically attended adverse events (AEs), AEs of special interest, and AEs resulting in withdrawal were collected through day 28. Participants were followed until day 720.
Main results
At least one local reaction was reported after 786 (75%) doses and at least one systemic reaction after 798 (76%) doses. Local reactogenicity ranged from 47% (110/236) with NVX-CoV2373 (Nvx) to 90% (131/145) with Moderna, while systemic reactogenicity ranged from 69% (162/236) with Nvx to 85% (121/143) with BNT162b2. Severe reactogenicity events were rare.
Interpretation and novelty
Reactogenicity events were common but rarely severe. Although reactogenicity appeared less frequent following Nvx than mRNA vaccines within strata, the trial was not powered for between-vaccine comparisons and findings should be interpreted descriptively.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide descriptive insights into the reactogenicity profiles of various licensed vaccines, which can inform future vaccine development and administration strategies for immunocompetent adults.
Limitations and evidence strength
Reactogenicity events were recorded on days 1-7, and serious adverse events were collected through day 28, which may limit the assessment of long-term effects.

Journal: PLoS Medicine  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-02  |  DOI: 10.1371/journal.pmed.1005230  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, e1005230
A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

Neutralising antibodies and protection from progression to severe COVID-19: A meta-analysis.

Neutralising antibodies and protection from progression to severe COVID-19: A meta-analysis.
作者： Karen M Elias, Ainslie Mitchell, Eva Stadler, Timothy E. Schlub, Shanchita R. Khan, Claire Xue, Stephen J. Kent, Deborah Cromer, Miles P. Davenport, David S. Khoury
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review investigates the relationship between vaccine-induced neutralising antibodies and protection from progression to severe COVID-19, focusing on hospital or ICU admission. The central question is whether neutralising antibodies are a correlate of protection against severe outcomes and their potential mechanistic role.
证据基础与方法
The review is a meta-analysis that systematically searched the literature for estimates of vaccine protection against progression to severe COVID-19. It aggregated results from 25 published clinical studies and matched them with data from 301 studies of post-vaccination neutralisation titres against different SARS-CoV-2 variants. The data were synthesised using meta-regression, and the main methodological limitations included the fact that neutralisation titres and clinical outcomes were measured in different studies, and the possibility of unmeasured confounding.
共识与结论
The review found that neutralising antibody titres were associated with vaccine protection against progression to severe COVID-19, with a relative risk (RR) of 0.57 per 10-fold increase in GMT, 95% CI [0.51, 0.64], p < 0.001. It also concluded that vaccine protection against progression to severe outcomes was higher than that provided by therapeutic passive administration of antibodies, suggesting that other immune responses may contribute to protection.
争议与缺口
There is a protection gap between the high levels of protection against progression to severe COVID-19 afforded by vaccination and that provided by therapeutic administration of antibodies. This suggests that other immune responses may also play a role. Additionally, the review notes that neutralisation titres and clinical outcomes were measured in different studies, which may introduce heterogeneity and limit the strength of the evidence.
科研与实践启示
这些发现强调了了解中和抗体在 疫苗 诱导的针对严重后果的保护中的作用的重要性。这些知识可以为未来的 疫苗 开发提供信息,并可以指导更有效的治疗策略的设计。需要进一步研究来表征保护机制并探索其他 免疫应答 的贡献。

Neutralising antibodies and protection from progression to severe COVID-19: A meta-analysis.

Authors: Karen M Elias, Ainslie Mitchell, Eva Stadler, Timothy E. Schlub, Shanchita R. Khan, Claire Xue, Stephen J. Kent, Deborah Cromer, Miles P. Davenport, David S. Khoury
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Vaccine-induced neutralising antibodies are a well-established correlate of protection against acquisition of COVID-19. Vaccine protection from severe COVID-19 remains high, although the role of neutralising antibodies and the contribution of other immune responses to protection from severe outcomes is less clear. Improving understanding of how vaccines prevent severe outcomes is important for future vaccine development. METHODS AND FINDINGS: Here we undertake a systematic search of the literature to obtain estimates of vaccine protection against progression from SARS-CoV-2 infection to severe COVID-19 (hospital or ICU admission). We aggregate results from 25 published clinical studies of vaccine protection from progression to severe outcomes in people with SARS-CoV-2 infection. We match this clinical data to data from 301 studies of post-vaccination neutralisation titres induced by various vaccines against different SARS-CoV-2 variants from the publicly available Stanford University Coronavirus antiviral and resistance database. We then synthesise the data using meta-regression. Neutralising antibody titres were found to be associated with vaccine protection against progression to severe COVID-19 (relative risk (RR) 0.57 per 10-fold increase in GMT, 95% CI [0.51,0.64], p < 0.001). We found that the protection against progression provided by vaccination was higher than that provided by therapeutic passive administration of antibodies, when used to treat individuals with confirmed infection. The main methodological limitations were the fact that neutralisation titres and clinical outcomes were measured in different studies, and the possibility of unmeasured confounding. CONCLUSIONS: Together, this work suggests that vaccine-induced neutralising antibodies are correlated with protection from progression to severe COVID-19 and can account for at least a partial mechanistic role in this protection. However, a protection gap remains between the high levels of protection against progressing to severe COVID-19 afforded by vaccination, compared to that afforded by therapeutic administration of antibodies, suggesting other immune responses may also play a role.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review investigates the relationship between vaccine-induced neutralising antibodies and protection from progression to severe COVID-19, focusing on hospital or ICU admission. The central question is whether neutralising antibodies are a correlate of protection against severe outcomes and their potential mechanistic role.
Evidence base and method
The review is a meta-analysis that systematically searched the literature for estimates of vaccine protection against progression to severe COVID-19. It aggregated results from 25 published clinical studies and matched them with data from 301 studies of post-vaccination neutralisation titres against different SARS-CoV-2 variants. The data were synthesised using meta-regression, and the main methodological limitations included the fact that neutralisation titres and clinical outcomes were measured in different studies, and the possibility of unmeasured confounding.
Consensus and conclusions
The review found that neutralising antibody titres were associated with vaccine protection against progression to severe COVID-19, with a relative risk (RR) of 0.57 per 10-fold increase in GMT, 95% CI [0.51, 0.64], p < 0.001. It also concluded that vaccine protection against progression to severe outcomes was higher than that provided by therapeutic passive administration of antibodies, suggesting that other immune responses may contribute to protection.
Controversies and gaps
There is a protection gap between the high levels of protection against progression to severe COVID-19 afforded by vaccination and that provided by therapeutic administration of antibodies. This suggests that other immune responses may also play a role. Additionally, the review notes that neutralisation titres and clinical outcomes were measured in different studies, which may introduce heterogeneity and limit the strength of the evidence.
Research and practice implications
The findings highlight the importance of understanding the role of neutralising antibodies in vaccine-induced protection against severe outcomes. This knowledge can inform future vaccine development and may guide the design of more effective therapeutic strategies. Further research is needed to characterise the mechanisms of protection and to explore the contribution of other immune responses.

Recommendations for COVID-19 Vaccines in Infants, Children, and Adolescents, 2026-2027: Policy Statement

Authors: Committee on Infectious Diseases.
Original abstract (English)
This policy statement updates the recommendations of the American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) for the use of COVID-19 vaccines in the prevention of severe COVID-19 in children. A review of evidence supporting these recommendations is in the accompanying technical report (https://doi.org/10.1542/peds.2026-079046). These COVID-19 vaccine recommendations may change in future seasons or as additional variants emerge. The AAP recommends all infants and children 6 through 23 months of age who do not have contraindications receive 2026–2027 COVID-19 vaccine. The AAP also recommends a single dose of age-appropriate 2026–2027 COVID-19 vaccine for all children and adolescents 2 through 18 years of age at increased risk of severe COVID-19, regardless of prior COVID-19 vaccination status. Children 2 through 18 years of age not considered at increased risk of severe COVID-19 whose parent or guardian desires their protection from COVID-19 should be offered a single dose of age-appropriate 2026–2027 COVID-19 vaccine. Any available COVID-19 vaccine appropriate by age and health status can be administered and the most updated version of the COVID-19 vaccine that is available should be used.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on SARS-CoV-2, specifically addressing the prevention of severe COVID-19 in children through vaccination. The central question is about the appropriate use of vaccines in infants, children, and adolescents, including recommendations for different age groups and risk categories.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base is supported by an accompanying technical report (https://doi.org/10.1542/peds.2026-079046). The review includes a systematic analysis of available data, with recommendations based on the most updated vaccine formulations and their efficacy in preventing severe outcomes in children.
Consensus and conclusions
The AAP recommends that all infants and children 6 through 23 months of age without contraindications receive the 2026–2027 COVID-19 vaccine. Additionally, children and adolescents 2 through 18 years of age at increased risk of severe COVID-19 should receive a single dose of the age-appropriate vaccine, regardless of prior vaccination status. Those not at increased risk but seeking protection should also be offered a single dose.
Controversies and gaps
The AAP acknowledges that recommendations may change in future seasons or with the emergence of new variants. There is no explicit mention of evidence gaps or controversies in the review, but the potential for future changes suggests ongoing uncertainty and the need for further research.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the importance of continued surveillance and updates to vaccine formulations as new variants emerge. It also emphasizes the need for ongoing research to refine recommendations for different age groups and risk categories, particularly in light of the potential for future changes.

Hospital system access to capital and the COVID-19 pandemic

Hospital system access to capital and the COVID-19 pandemic
作者： Sung-Keun Choi, Jeffrey S. McCullough

This study examines the impact of COVID-19 on U.S. hospital systems' access to capital through the municipal bond market.
证据基础与方法
使用电子市政市场准入系统中与美国医院协会年度调查(2019 年 4 月至 2021 年 2 月)相匹配的医院债券发行数据,我们分析了医院借款数量、成本和构成的变化。

医院债券发行总量下降34%,其中发行数量下降13%,单只债券规模缩小23%。尽管出现这些收缩,借贷成本仍保持稳定,这可能是由于美联储政策和新冠疫情救助基金的缘故。

Hospital system access to capital and the COVID-19 pandemic

Authors: Sung-Keun Choi, Jeffrey S. McCullough
Original abstract (English)
This study examines the impact of COVID-19 on U.S. hospital systems' access to capital through the municipal bond market. Using hospital bond issuance data from the Electronic Municipal Market Access system matched to the American Hospital Association Annual Survey (April 2019 to February 2021), we analyze changes in the volume, cost, and composition of hospital borrowing. The total quantity of hospital bond issuances declined by 34%, with the number of issuances dropping by 13% and individual bond sizes shrinking by 23%. Despite these contractions, borrowing costs remained stable, likely due to Federal Reserve policies and COVID relief funds. The composition of borrowers shifted notably: smaller and rural hospital systems increasingly relied on external capital, while larger systems demonstrated greater financial resilience. State-level COVID-19 incidence was not significantly associated with bond market outcomes, and selection correction models confirmed that findings were not driven by selection bias. Policy interventions helped stabilize hospital capital markets, but concerns persist regarding long-term debt sustainability, particularly for smaller and rural hospital systems that may require continued financial support.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study examines the impact of COVID-19 on U.S. hospital systems' access to capital through the municipal bond market.
Evidence base and method
Using hospital bond issuance data from the Electronic Municipal Market Access system matched to the American Hospital Association Annual Survey (April 2019 to February 2021), we analyze changes in the volume, cost, and composition of hospital borrowing.
Consensus and conclusions
The total quantity of hospital bond issuances declined by 34%, with the number of issuances dropping by 13% and individual bond sizes shrinking by 23%. Despite these contractions, borrowing costs remained stable, likely due to Federal Reserve policies and COVID relief funds.
Controversies and gaps
State-level COVID-19 incidence was not significantly associated with bond market outcomes, and selection correction models confirmed that findings were not driven by selection bias.
Research and practice implications
Policy interventions helped stabilize hospital capital markets, but concerns persist regarding long-term debt sustainability, particularly for smaller and rural hospital systems that may require continued financial support.

大流行期间小岛社会的研究——关于严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2大流行期间法罗岛研究贡献的叙述性综述。

Pandemic research in a small island society-a narrative review of research contributions during the SARS-CoV-2 pandemic in the Faroe Islands.
作者： Gunnhild Helmsdal, Maria Skaalum Petersen, Marnar Fríðheim Kristiansen
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 在法罗群岛的影响,研究大流行期间对个人的流行病学、免疫学和心理影响。核心问题是,小型、孤立的社会如何在大流行期间进行研究,以及这为理解 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 提供了哪些见解。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一篇叙述性文献综述,包括 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 大流行期间在法罗群岛进行的流行病学、免疫学和心理学研究。它不报告荟萃分析或汇总估计,而是报告个别研究及其影响。
共识与结论
在法罗群岛进行的研究表明,拥有强大基础设施的小型、有凝聚力的社会可以在大流行期间进行高质量的研究。这些研究涵盖 传播、免疫、心理健康和 长新冠,为当地和全球知识做出了贡献。
争议与缺口
该评论指出,虽然法罗群岛的人口进行了广泛的研究,但在了解 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 及其变体的长期影响方面仍然存在差距。此外,该审查强调需要对心理健康和大流行后状况进行更详细的研究。
科研与实践启示
审查建议,进一步的研究应重点关注 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2(特别是 长新冠)对健康的长期影响,以及小岛屿社会的心理健康结果。它还强调了在未来的健康危机期间在孤立人群中维持强大的 监测 和研究基础设施的重要性。

Pandemic research in a small island society-a narrative review of research contributions during the SARS-CoV-2 pandemic in the Faroe Islands.

Authors: Gunnhild Helmsdal, Maria Skaalum Petersen, Marnar Fríðheim Kristiansen
Original abstract (English)
Small and geographically isolated societies face distinct challenges during a pandemic but may also possess advantages. This narrative literature review presents epidemiological, immunological and psychological studies conducted in the Faroe Islands during the SARS-CoV-2 pandemic, examining its effects on individuals in the Faroe Islands. The COVID-19 pandemic hit the Faroe Islands early in March 2020. With only around 55,000 inhabitants, the Faroe Islands' isolated, well-documented population enabled extensive epidemiological research and close monitoring of infection patterns. This resulted in studies on transmission, immunity, mental health and long COVID. Overall, the Faroese studies demonstrate how small, cohesive societies with robust infrastructure can conduct high-quality research during a crisis, contributing to both local and global knowledge.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the effects of SARS-CoV-2 in the Faroe Islands, examining epidemiological, immunological, and psychological impacts on individuals during the pandemic. The central question is how small, isolated societies can conduct research during a pandemic and what insights this provides for understanding SARS-CoV-2.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative literature review that includes epidemiological, immunological, and psychological studies conducted in the Faroe Islands during the SARS-CoV-2 pandemic. It does not report on meta-analysis or pooled estimates, but rather on individual studies and their implications.
Consensus and conclusions
The studies conducted in the Faroe Islands demonstrate that small, cohesive societies with robust infrastructure can perform high-quality research during a pandemic. These studies cover transmission, immunity, mental health, and long COVID, contributing to both local and global knowledge.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that while the Faroe Islands' population enabled extensive research, there are still gaps in understanding the long-term effects of SARS-CoV-2 and its variants. Additionally, the review highlights the need for more detailed studies on mental health and post-pandemic conditions.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that further research should focus on the long-term health effects of SARS-CoV-2, particularly long COVID, and on mental health outcomes in small island societies. It also emphasizes the importance of maintaining robust surveillance and research infrastructure in isolated populations during future health crises.

Factors that impact on healthcare workers' adherence with infection prevention and control (IPC) guidelines for COVID-19: a qualitative evidence synthesis.

Factors that impact on healthcare workers' adherence with infection prevention and control (IPC) guidelines for COVID-19: a qualitative evidence synthesis.
作者： Catherine Houghton, P. Meskell, Hannah Delaney, Sarah E. Sheehan, Cian O. Donoghue, C. Glenton, A. Booth, G. Jorem, Xin-Hui S. Chan, D. Devane
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
重要的是要研究大流行的背景如何影响未来大流行情况下医护人员 (HCW) 遵守 IPC 指南的能力。
证据基础与方法
出于及时性和相关性的考虑,我们仅纳入 2020 年以来发表的研究。
共识与结论
我们确定了 27 项调查结果,概述了影响医护人员遵守 IPC 指南的因素。
争议与缺口
被认为能够引起大流行的传染性呼吸道病原体(例如新型流感亚型或冠状病毒)。
科研与实践启示
感染预防和控制 (IPC) 指南对于提供有关使用个人防护装备 (PPE)、将呼吸道感染患者与其他患者分开以及更严格的清洁程序的策略非常重要。

Factors that impact on healthcare workers' adherence with infection prevention and control (IPC) guidelines for COVID-19: a qualitative evidence synthesis.

Authors: Catherine Houghton, P. Meskell, Hannah Delaney, Sarah E. Sheehan, Cian O. Donoghue, C. Glenton, A. Booth, G. Jorem, Xin-Hui S. Chan, D. Devane
Original abstract (English)
Background The COVID-19 pandemic, declared in 2020, brought unprecedented challenges to our healthcare services in relation to the prevention and treatment of the disease. Additionally, preventing transmission within healthcare settings was critical. Infection Prevention and Control (IPC) guidelines are important for providing strategies on the use of personal protective equipment (PPE), the separation of patients with respiratory infections from others, and stricter cleaning routines. It is important to examine how the context of the pandemic impacts healthcare workers' (HCW) ability to adhere to IPC guidance in pandemic situations in the future. This is an update of an earlier review version, published in 2020, titled 'Barriers and facilitators to healthcare workers' adherence with infection prevention and control (IPC) guidelines for respiratory infectious diseases: a rapid qualitative evidence synthesis'. We built on available evidence for infectious respiratory diseases, but also importantly, given the unprecedented global scale of COVID-19 and its unique challenges for healthcare systems, explored additional factors influencing IPC adherence specific to respiratory diseases with pandemic potential, i.e. infectious respiratory pathogens (such as novel influenza subtypes or coronaviruses) judged capable of causing a pandemic. Objectives Our primary objective is to identify the factors that impact on HCWs' adherence to IPC guidelines for COVID-19. A second objective is to update an existing rapid review, published in 2020, to identify similarities, differences, and novel insights specific to COVID-19 compared with other respiratory infectious diseases. Search methods We searched MEDLINE (Ovid), CINAHL (EBSCO), Scopus, Ovid PsycINFO (EBSCO), and Epistemonikos. We did not apply any language limits but only searched from 2020 to the present (last search 5 April 2024). In the interest of timeliness and relevance, we only included studies published from 2020. We chose this as it marks the year when the COVID-19 pandemic was declared. Selection criteria We included qualitative and mixed methods studies that focused on the experiences and perceptions of HCWs towards factors that impact on their ability to adhere to IPC guidelines for COVID-19. We included studies of any type of healthcare worker with responsibility for patient care. We included studies that focused on IPC guidelines (local, national, or international) for COVID-19 in any healthcare setting. We excluded studies that collected data using qualitative methods (e.g. open-ended survey questions) in which the response data were analysed only with descriptive statistics. Data collection and analysis We used a purposive sampling frame to identify data-rich studies that represented a range of HCWs, healthcare settings, and geographical spread. We assessed methodological strengths and limitations in the same studies using an adapted version of the Critical Skills Appraisal Programme (CASP) tool for qualitative studies. We used the 'best-fit framework approach' to analyse and synthesise the evidence from our included studies. We used the GRADE-CERQual (Confidence in the Evidence from Reviews of Qualitative research) approach to assess our confidence in each finding. We examined each review finding to identify factors that may influence guideline adherence and developed implications for practice. Main results We found 170 studies eligible for inclusion; of these, 26 were sampled for analysis using a purposive sampling frame for a balance of variation and richness of data. Seven of the 26 sampled studies were from high-income countries. Of the 15 middle-income countries, seven were upper middle-income countries, and eight were lower middle-income countries. There were four low-income countries included in the sample. In terms of geographical spread, ten were from Asia, eight from Africa, three were from North America, one from South America, one from Oceania, and three from Europe. Most of the studies included nurses (15 studies) or doctors (11 studies). Other types of healthcare workers included in the studies were other clinical staff (11 studies), such as midwives, allied health professionals, medical and radiologic technologists, dental professionals, neonatologists, radiographers, pharmacists, and support staff (7 studies), such as hospital cleaners, clerical staff, technicians, and hospital attendants. Healthcare settings ranged across and within studies, including hospitals, long-term care settings, and community healthcare settings. Specific units such as maternity, intensive care, emergency care, critical care, and operating rooms were identified in five of the sampled studies. We identified 27 findings that outline the factors that impact on HCWs' adherence to IPC guidelines. We have low (n = 3), moderate (n = 12), and high (n = 12) confidence in these findings. HCWs found it easier to follow IPC guidelines with managerial support, and when IPC guidelines were communicated in a clear, structured, and accessible way. They found it more difficult to follow guidelines if they were busy, short-staffed, or when patients and visitors refused to wear masks and maintain social distancing. Training was considered very important, but not all HCWs had the opportunity to attend IPC training. In terms of the working environment, HCWs need adequate space, hand-washing stations, and showers to follow the IPC guidelines. HCWs worried about infecting other people, but some worried less once people started being vaccinated. It was very important that all HCWs had sufficient and equitable access to PPE and other supplies to follow guidelines. If PPE did not fit properly or was not of good quality, it was more difficult for HCWs to practice IPC. Many factors affect HCWs' willingness and ability to follow IPC guidelines. Our review includes a set of questions based on our findings to help healthcare providers plan, implement, or manage IPC strategies to help their workers follow IPC guidelines for pandemic diseases like COVID-19. It is worth noting that our included studies were conducted primarily during the earlier phase of the pandemic, based on practical considerations to not update the search after April 2024. Authors' conclusions We identified several factors that influence the ability of healthcare workers to adhere to IPC guidelines. Practical implications, such as communication strategies and the provision of training and supplies, should guide policymakers and decision-makers in disease outbreaks and future pandemics. Funding This Cochrane review had no dedicated funding. Registration This Cochrane review extends the work by a published rapid review, available via DOI: 10.1002/14651858.CD013582.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
It is important to examine how the context of the pandemic impacts healthcare workers' (HCW) ability to adhere to IPC guidance in pandemic situations in the future.
Evidence base and method
In the interest of timeliness and relevance, we only included studies published from 2020.
Consensus and conclusions
We identified 27 findings that outline the factors that impact on HCWs' adherence to IPC guidelines.
Controversies and gaps
infectious respiratory pathogens (such as novel influenza subtypes or coronaviruses) judged capable of causing a pandemic.
Research and practice implications
Infection Prevention and Control (IPC) guidelines are important for providing strategies on the use of personal protective equipment (PPE), the separation of patients with respiratory infections from others, and stricter cleaning routines.

Urban green spaces: lessons from COVID-19 for future public health crises.

Urban green spaces: lessons from COVID-19 for future public health crises.
作者： Natália Cristina de Oliveira, Giovanni Sanesi, T. D. de Paiva, Alvim DS, Danúbia Caporusso Bargos, C. F. Fontana, Sidnei Aranha, Raffale Lafortezza, M. L. Ferreira
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review focuses on the role of urban green spaces (UGS) during the COVID-19 pandemic, examining how the virus's spread and containment measures influenced UGS usage and access. The central question is how UGS can be leveraged to support public health during future crises, with a specific emphasis on SARS-CoV-2 and its variants.
证据基础与方法
该综述对 2020 年至 2025 年间发表的 155 项研究进行了系统分析,分为四个领域:访问模式、UGS 的规划和管理、与病毒 传播 的关联以及健康益处。这些研究根据其研究重点进行分组,综述旨在综合这些领域的研究结果。
共识与结论
审查发现,大流行显着改变了UGS的访问模式,靠近居民区成为户外活动的关键因素。它还强调了获得 UGS 的持续不平等,特别是在低收入社区、少数民族和社会弱势群体中。
争议与缺口
关于 UGS 和病毒 传播 之间直接关系的证据有限,该评论指出,经验教训的适用性取决于病原体特征、城市形态和治理能力等因素。此外,审查还发现了在理解如何将 UGS 有效纳入应急准备策略方面的差距。
科研与实践启示
审查建议确保公平获得高质量的地下资源系统,改善其空间分布和质量,将其纳入应急准备,实施环境敏感的管理协议,并支持绿色空间中促进健康的行为。

Urban green spaces: lessons from COVID-19 for future public health crises.

Authors: Natália Cristina de Oliveira, Giovanni Sanesi, T. D. de Paiva, Alvim DS, Danúbia Caporusso Bargos, C. F. Fontana, Sidnei Aranha, Raffale Lafortezza, M. L. Ferreira
Original abstract (English)
The COVID-19 pandemic constituted a global socioeconomic crisis with long-lasting consequences. In response to the rapid spread of the virus, governments implemented containment measures such as social distancing, mask mandates, and mobility restrictions, which also affected access to urban green spaces (UGS). This systematic review aimed to identify key lessons from the pandemic regarding the role of UGS during public health crises. The analysis included 155 studies published between 2020 and 2025, grouped into four main research domains: visitation patterns (n=88), planning and management of UGS (n=15), associations with viral transmission (n=11), and health benefits (n=41). The findings indicated substantial geographical disparities in scientific production and showed that the pandemic reshaped UGS visitation patterns, with proximity to residential areas becoming an important determinant of outdoor activities such as physical exercise and recreation. The literature also revealed persistent inequalities in access to urban nature, particularly among low-income communities, ethnic minorities, and other socially vulnerable groups. Drawing on different forms of evidence, five policy recommendations emerge: i) ensuring equitable access to UGS; ii) improving their quality and spatial distribution; iii) integrating UGS into emergency preparedness strategies; iv) implementing context-sensitive visitation and management protocols; and v) actively supporting health-promoting behaviors compatible with safe use of green environments. The applicability of these lessons to future crises will depend on pathogen characteristics, transmission routes, urban form, restriction policies, and governance capacity. The findings reinforce that equitable access to high-quality UGS should be recognized as a strategic priority for urban governance and public health resilience.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the role of urban green spaces (UGS) during the COVID-19 pandemic, examining how the virus's spread and containment measures influenced UGS usage and access. The central question is how UGS can be leveraged to support public health during future crises, with a specific emphasis on SARS-CoV-2 and its variants.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of 155 studies published between 2020 and 2025, categorized into four domains: visitation patterns, planning and management of UGS, associations with viral transmission, and health benefits. The studies were grouped based on their research focus, and the review aimed to synthesize findings across these domains.
Consensus and conclusions
The review found that the pandemic significantly altered UGS visitation patterns, with proximity to residential areas becoming a key factor in outdoor activities. It also highlighted persistent inequalities in access to UGS, particularly among low-income communities, ethnic minorities, and socially vulnerable groups.
Controversies and gaps
There is limited evidence on the direct relationship between UGS and viral transmission, and the review notes that the applicability of lessons learned depends on factors such as pathogen characteristics, urban form, and governance capacity. Additionally, the review identifies gaps in understanding how UGS can be effectively integrated into emergency preparedness strategies.
Research and practice implications
The review recommends ensuring equitable access to high-quality UGS, improving their spatial distribution and quality, integrating them into emergency preparedness, implementing context-sensitive management protocols, and supporting health-promoting behaviors in green spaces.

The Role of Community Health Workers in Increasing Access to Essential Health and Social Services During the COVID-19 Pandemic.

The Role of Community Health Workers in Increasing Access to Essential Health and Social Services During the COVID-19 Pandemic.
作者： T. T. Jiri-Beta, Katie C. Stalker, Mihaly Imre, Ada M. Wilkinson-Lee, Natalie Truesdell, Mary-Ellen Brown, Fox DJ, Jennifer Rubio, Flavio F. Marsiglia
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review aims to describe the health care and social services needs addressed by community health workers (CHWs) through referrals in the Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) program. The focus is on SARS-CoV-2, COVID-19, and named SARS-CoV-2 variants, with the goal of understanding how CHWs contribute to addressing these needs.
证据基础与方法
该审查对 2021 年至 2024 年 67 个 CCR 接受者的国家绩效衡量数据进行了描述性分析,记录了转介情况,同时还于 2024 年初对美国 63 名计划管理员和 689 名社区卫生工作者进行了 2 次网络调查。数据来源包括定量绩效衡量和定性调查答复。
共识与结论
社区卫生工作者进行了 464,158 次社会转诊和 323,769 次健康转诊,已完成 282,596 次转诊。这些发现支持社区卫生工作者在将个人与医疗保健联系起来并提供后续护理和社会支持方面的作用,强调了全面和协调的健康和社会服务在优化转诊和服务提供方面的重要性。
争议与缺口
转介成功与否受到社区资源、社区卫生工作者知识和网络的影响,但也受到成本、不信任、语言障碍、交通和儿童保育挑战的限制。这些限制表明某些人群或地区社区卫生工作者服务的可及性和有效性方面存在潜在差距。
科研与实践启示
研究结果支持促进社区卫生工作者劳动力整合、扩张和可持续性的政策。公共卫生影响包括需要进一步研究和实践,以解决已发现的障碍并提高社区卫生工作者计划的有效性。

The Role of Community Health Workers in Increasing Access to Essential Health and Social Services During the COVID-19 Pandemic.

Authors: T. T. Jiri-Beta, Katie C. Stalker, Mihaly Imre, Ada M. Wilkinson-Lee, Natalie Truesdell, Mary-Ellen Brown, Fox DJ, Jennifer Rubio, Flavio F. Marsiglia
Original abstract (English)
To describe the health care and social services needs addressed by community health workers (CHWs) through referrals in the Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) program. We performed a descriptive analysis of national performance measure data from 67 CCR recipients from 2021 to 2024 documenting referrals, alongside 2 web-based surveys of 63 program administrators and 689 CHWs in the United States in early 2024. CHWs made 464 158 social and 323 769 health referrals, with 282 596 referrals completed. Referrals included services for food, housing, legal assistance, health insurance, lifestyle interventions, chronic disease management, COVID-19‒related care, and maternal and child health care. Referral success was aided by community resources, CHW knowledge, and networking, and limited by costs, mistrust, language barriers, transportation, and child care challenges. CHWs connect individuals to health care and provide follow-up care and social support. Comprehensive and coordinated health and social services are essential to optimize the referral and service delivery. Public Health Implications. Findings support policies promoting the integration, expansion, and sustainability of the CHW workforce. ( Am J Public Health . 2026;116(S4):S331-S340. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308642).
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review aims to describe the health care and social services needs addressed by community health workers (CHWs) through referrals in the Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) program. The focus is on SARS-CoV-2, COVID-19, and named SARS-CoV-2 variants, with the goal of understanding how CHWs contribute to addressing these needs.
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a descriptive analysis of national performance measure data from 67 CCR recipients from 2021 to 2024 documenting referrals, alongside 2 web-based surveys of 63 program administrators and 689 CHWs in the United States in early 2024. The data sources include both quantitative performance measures and qualitative survey responses.
Consensus and conclusions
CHWs made 464,158 social and 323,769 health referrals, with 282,596 referrals completed. These findings support the role of CHWs in connecting individuals to health care and providing follow-up care and social support, highlighting the importance of comprehensive and coordinated health and social services in optimizing referral and service delivery.
Controversies and gaps
Referral success was influenced by community resources, CHW knowledge, and networking, but was also limited by costs, mistrust, language barriers, transportation, and child care challenges. These limitations suggest potential gaps in the accessibility and effectiveness of CHW services in certain populations or regions.
Research and practice implications
The findings support policies promoting the integration, expansion, and sustainability of the CHW workforce. Public health implications include the need for further research and practice to address the barriers identified and to enhance the effectiveness of CHW programs.

The Impact of Setting Characteristics on Community Health Worker Roles and COVID-19 Activities: An Examination of the Community Health Workers for COVID Response and Resilient Communities Program.

Authors: Katie C. Stalker, Mary-Ellen Brown, N. Wiggins, Victoria Adewumi, Sahlea Tubbeh, Flavio F. Marsiglia, Floribella Redondo-Martinez, C. Ibe, Ada M. Wilkinson-Lee
Original abstract (English)
To examine the roles and activities performed by community health workers (CHWs) involved in the national Community Health Workers for COVID Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) program and examine differences based on setting characteristics. We used data from a CHW self-report survey (n = 689) conducted February through March 2024 in the United States. We ran a series of ordered logistic regression models to examine the association between setting characteristics (i.e., type of organizational setting, organizational experience with CHWs, and populations of focus) and CHW core roles and COVID-19‒specific activities. A total of 69.2% of CHWs in the analytic sample reported performing all 10 core roles. Organizational experience with CHWs and populations of focus emerged as the most salient predictors of CHW core roles and activities. A substantial proportion of CHWs performed the full range of core roles. Results suggest that CHWs in the CCR program tailored their roles and activities according to the unique factors of the communities in which they worked. Public Health Implications. Programs such as CCR may result in CHWs using a wide range of core roles across multiple settings. ( Am J Public Health . 2026;116(S4):S295-S304. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308660).

Five Year Recovery From COVID-19 Smell Loss: Preliminary Report from a Nationwide Survey.

Five Year Recovery From COVID-19 Smell Loss: Preliminary Report from a Nationwide Survey.
作者： Aaron Tucker, Reiter ER, Costanzo RM, Coelho DH
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This scientific briefing reports preliminary 5-year recovery outcomes from COVID-19-associated smell loss in a nationwide cohort.
证据基础与方法
Adults with confirmed COVID-19 infection and self-reported smell loss since January 2020 were surveyed through a longitudinal web-based questionnaire, and a follow-up survey was distributed to participants who had completed a prior 2-year study.
共识与结论
Among 419 respondents (mean age 45.7 ± 13.7 years; 79.0% female; 84.5% white), 44.2% reported complete smell recovery, 48.4% partial recovery, and 7.4% no recovery or worsening smell. These findings indicate that while modest additional recovery occurs beyond 2 years, olfactory dysfunction persists in more than half of patients 5 years after COVID-19-associated smell loss.
争议与缺口
40 岁以下参与者的完全康复率显着较高 (P < .001),但性别之间没有差异 (P = .51)。与2年结果相比,完全康复率从38.4%提高到44.3%。
科研与实践启示
虽然目前正在进行更大规模的分析,但该报告代表了迄今为止发布的最长期结果。

Five Year Recovery From COVID-19 Smell Loss: Preliminary Report from a Nationwide Survey.

Authors: Aaron Tucker, Reiter ER, Costanzo RM, Coelho DH
Original abstract (English)
This scientific briefing reports preliminary 5-year recovery outcomes from COVID-19-associated smell loss in a nationwide cohort. Adults with confirmed COVID-19 infection and self-reported smell loss since January 2020 were surveyed through a longitudinal web-based questionnaire, and a follow-up survey was distributed to participants who had completed a prior 2-year study. Among 419 respondents (mean age 45.7 ± 13.7 years; 79.0% female; 84.5% white), 44.2% reported complete smell recovery, 48.4% partial recovery, and 7.4% no recovery or worsening smell. Complete recovery was significantly higher in participants younger than 40 years (P < .001) but did not differ by gender (P = .51). Compared with 2-year outcomes, the rate of complete recovery increased from 38.4% to 44.3%. These findings indicate that while modest additional recovery occurs beyond 2 years, olfactory dysfunction persists in more than half of patients 5 years after COVID-19-associated smell loss. While a much larger analysis is currently in progress, this report represents the longest-term results published to date.
View review five-element analysis

Consensus and conclusions

These findings indicate that while modest additional recovery occurs beyond 2 years, olfactory dysfunction persists in more than half of patients 5 years after COVID-19-associated smell loss.

Complete recovery was significantly higher in participants younger than 40 years (P < .001) but did not differ by gender (P = .51). Compared with 2-year outcomes, the rate of complete recovery increased from 38.4% to 44.3%.

Evaluating the Potential Influence of Federal COVID-19 Response Funding on the Building and Sustainability of the CHW Workforce.

Evaluating the Potential Influence of Federal COVID-19 Response Funding on the Building and Sustainability of the CHW Workforce.
作者： T. T. Jiri-Beta, Terry Greene, Yu Fu Michael Chen, Marianne Mabida, R. McCloud, Fox DJ, Margaret Frankhouser, Alina Bardwell, N. Wiggins
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review aims to evaluate the potential influence of Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) funding on community health worker (CHW) hiring, retention, and workforce sustainability.
证据基础与方法
本研究通过在美国开展的一项横断面网络调查,对63个CCR受资助机构的主管与协调员进行了描述性分析,调查时间为2024年2月至3月。
共识与结论
Survey questions assessed program funding sources, payment mechanisms for CHW services, and CHW hiring and retention. About 65% of CCR recipients relied solely on CCR funds, whereas others leveraged over $117 million in additional funding, mostly federal, to support CCR program implementation. Of 1359 CHW positions supported by CCR, respondents expected to maintain 749.
争议与缺口
研究结果强调了通过多元化资金来源进行长期投资和建设规划能力以加强和维持社区卫生工作者队伍的重要性。
科研与实践启示
联邦资金可以为维持不同环境下的社区卫生工作者就业作出有意义的贡献,但这一重要公共卫生劳动力的可持续性并不能得到保证。

Evaluating the Potential Influence of Federal COVID-19 Response Funding on the Building and Sustainability of the CHW Workforce.

Authors: T. T. Jiri-Beta, Terry Greene, Yu Fu Michael Chen, Marianne Mabida, R. McCloud, Fox DJ, Margaret Frankhouser, Alina Bardwell, N. Wiggins
Original abstract (English)
To describe the potential influence of Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) funding on community health worker (CHW) hiring, retention, and workforce sustainability. A descriptive analysis of a cross-sectional, web-based survey of directors and coordinators of 63 CCR recipient organizations administered between February and March 2024 was conducted in the United States. Survey questions assessed program funding sources, payment mechanisms for CHW services, and CHW hiring and retention. About 65% of CCR recipients relied solely on CCR funds, whereas others leveraged over $117 million in additional funding, mostly federal, to support CCR program implementation. Of 1359 CHW positions supported by CCR, respondents expected to maintain 749. Recipients planned to use grant funding and Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services reimbursement as strategies for retention. Federal funding can contribute meaningfully to sustaining CHW employment across different settings, yet sustainability of this vital public health workforce is not assured. Public Health Implications. The findings underscore the importance of long-term investment via diverse funding sources and building programmatic capacity to strengthen and sustain the CHW workforce. ( Am J Public Health . 2026;116(S4):S321-S330. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308646).

Controversies and gaps

A Community Health Worker-Led Model of Training and Technical Assistance to Support National Community Health Worker Program Implementation for COVID-19 Response: United States, 2021-2025.

A Community Health Worker-Led Model of Training and Technical Assistance to Support National Community Health Worker Program Implementation for COVID-19 Response: United States, 2021-2025.
作者： Lisa Renee Holderby-Fox, Kira Wortmann, Kelsey N. Witmeier, Theresa Mason, Jennifer Park-Mroch, Catherine Haywood, Julie Smithwick, Sherri L. Ohly, Caple EL, Soledad Bolden
摘要中文翻译为评估美国67个由疾病控制与预防中心资助的社区健康工作者(CHW)计划中培训和技术援助(TTA)的需求,并评估一个全国性的由CHW主导的TTA计划的覆盖范围,我们进行了研究。2021年12月至2022年1月期间,我们通过在线调查和与项目工作人员的访谈评估了TTA需求。我们利用研究结果来指导TTA内容和实施方式。2021年至2025年期间,我们收集了参与数据以评估项目的覆盖范围。在59名调查受访者和10个组织的代表中,资金获得者将招聘、项目实施、财务可持续性和同行学习确定为TTA的重点。基于这些发现,我们开发了网络研讨会、实践社区、领导力培训以及个性化的TTA。该计划覆盖了所有获得资金的机构,并产生了8824次参与者互动,其中54.7%为社区健康工作者。实施CHW项目的卫生部门表现出对CHW主导的TTA的显著需求和参与度。政策制定者应优先考虑对TTA基础设施的持续投资,以支持CHW项目的实施和长期可持续性。( Am J Public Health . 2026;116(S4):S314-S320. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308665).

A Community Health Worker-Led Model of Training and Technical Assistance to Support National Community Health Worker Program Implementation for COVID-19 Response: United States, 2021-2025.

Authors: Lisa Renee Holderby-Fox, Kira Wortmann, Kelsey N. Witmeier, Theresa Mason, Jennifer Park-Mroch, Catherine Haywood, Julie Smithwick, Sherri L. Ohly, Caple EL, Soledad Bolden
Original abstract (English)
To assess training and technical assistance (TTA) needs among 67 Centers for Disease Control and Prevention-funded community health worker (CHW) programs in the United States and to assess the reach of a national CHW-led TTA program designed to meet the needs identified. From December 2021 through January 2022, we assessed TTA needs through an online survey and interviews with program staff. We used findings to inform TTA content and delivery. From 2021 through 2025, we collected participation data to assess program reach. Among 59 survey respondents and representatives from 10 organizations, funding recipients identified hiring, program implementation, financial sustainability, and peer learning as priorities for TTA. Based on these findings, we developed webinars, communities of practice, leadership training, and individualized TTA. The program reached all funded recipients and generated 8824 participant encounters, 54.7% of whom were CHWs. Health departments implementing CHW programs demonstrated substantial demand for and engagement in CHW-led TTA. Policymakers should prioritize sustained investment in TTA infrastructure to support implementation and long-term sustainability of CHW programs. ( Am J Public Health . 2026;116(S4):S314-S320. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308665).
View review five-element analysis

Evidence base and method

Consensus and conclusions
Health departments implementing CHW programs demonstrated substantial demand for and engagement in CHW-led TTA.
Controversies and gaps

Research and practice implications

COVID-19 Vaccine Effectiveness and Safety for the 2026-2027 Respiratory Season.

COVID-19 Vaccine Effectiveness and Safety for the 2026-2027 Respiratory Season.
作者： Rachel A. Lipson, Emily Senerth, Michelle A. Watson, Harseerat Saini, Jordan Falk, Neha Tangri, Seyyedeh Mahsa Sheikholeslamian, Kapeena Sivakumaran, Ifeoluwa Babatunde, Melinda Wharton
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This systematic review identifies and summarizes newly published studies reporting on the effectiveness and safety of COVID-19 vaccines and COVID-19 epidemiology in the US.
证据基础与方法
Cochrane Central Register of Controlled Trials, PubMed/MEDLINE, Embase, and Scopus were searched from August 1, 2025, through June 29, 2026. Studies were eligible if they reported on the effectiveness, efficacy, or safety of a US-licensed COVID-19 vaccine or SARS-CoV-2 epidemiology in the US.
共识与结论
Consistent with prior evidence, studies reported that updated COVID-19 vaccines were associated with a reduction in the risk of hospitalization among both older adults (≥65 years; vaccine effectiveness [VE], 53.0%; 95% CI, 37.0%-65.0%; 2025-2026 season) and adults (18-64 years; VE, 54.0%; 95% CI, 1.0%-78.0%; 2024-2025 season). Vaccination was also associated with a significant reduction in COVID-19-related pediatric emergency department visits (9 months to 4 years; VE, 76.0%; 95% CI, 58.0%-87.0%). Vaccination was associated with a reduced risk of critical COVID-19 illness (intensive care unit admission or death) in immunocompromised adults (VE range, 32.0%-53.0%, depending on condition and other factors).
争议与缺口
No new safety signals were reported in the recently published studies eligible for this updated review of US-licensed COVID-19 vaccines. No identified studies conducted under the updated guidance on extended dosing intervals for the primary series reported an increased risk of myocarditis or pericarditis.
科研与实践启示
Health care personnel who received 3 to 5 vaccine doses were less likely to develop laboratory-confirmed COVID-19 illness (VE, 41.0%; 95% CI, 34.0%-47.0%) and postacute COVID-19 symptoms (VE, 57.0%; 95% CI, 46.0%-66.0%) compared with those who received only 2 doses.

COVID-19 Vaccine Effectiveness and Safety for the 2026-2027 Respiratory Season.

Authors: Rachel A. Lipson, Emily Senerth, Michelle A. Watson, Harseerat Saini, Jordan Falk, Neha Tangri, Seyyedeh Mahsa Sheikholeslamian, Kapeena Sivakumaran, Ifeoluwa Babatunde, Melinda Wharton
Original abstract (English)
Importance: SARS-CoV-2 remains a substantial cause of respiratory illness, morbidity, and mortality. Evidence to date has demonstrated that COVID-19 vaccines reduce the risk of severe disease, including hospitalization and death. Objective: This systematic review identifies and summarizes newly published studies reporting on the effectiveness and safety of COVID-19 vaccines and COVID-19 epidemiology in the US. Evidence Review: Cochrane Central Register of Controlled Trials, PubMed/MEDLINE, Embase, and Scopus were searched from August 1, 2025, through June 29, 2026. Studies were eligible if they reported on the effectiveness, efficacy, or safety of a US-licensed COVID-19 vaccine or SARS-CoV-2 epidemiology in the US. Findings: From 11 829 identified references, 155 publications were eligible (15 randomized clinical trials, 84 observational studies with a comparator group, 38 product safety studies without a comparator group [single group], and 18 descriptive studies of disease burden). Consistent with prior evidence, studies reported that updated COVID-19 vaccines were associated with a reduction in the risk of hospitalization among both older adults (≥65 years; vaccine effectiveness [VE], 53.0%; 95% CI, 37.0%-65.0%; 2025-2026 season) and adults (18-64 years; VE, 54.0%; 95% CI, 1.0%-78.0%; 2024-2025 season). Maternal vaccination was associated with reduced risk of COVID-19-associated emergency department/urgent care encounters among individuals vaccinated (VE, 58.0%; 95% CI, 24.0%-77.0%) and with fewer COVID-19-related hospital contacts in the first 2 months of life among infants born subsequently (VE range, 50.0%-54.0%), regardless of trimester of vaccination. Vaccination was also associated with a significant reduction in COVID-19-related pediatric emergency department visits (9 months to 4 years; VE, 76.0%; 95% CI, 58.0%-87.0%). Vaccination was associated with a reduced risk of critical COVID-19 illness (intensive care unit admission or death) in immunocompromised adults (VE range, 32.0%-53.0%, depending on condition and other factors). Health care personnel who received 3 to 5 vaccine doses were less likely to develop laboratory-confirmed COVID-19 illness (VE, 41.0%; 95% CI, 34.0%-47.0%) and postacute COVID-19 symptoms (VE, 57.0%; 95% CI, 46.0%-66.0%) compared with those who received only 2 doses. Across adverse events of special interest (eg, stroke, thrombosis, myocarditis), safety profiles were consistent with those of previous reviews. No identified studies conducted under the updated guidance on extended dosing intervals for the primary series reported an increased risk of myocarditis or pericarditis. No new safety signals were reported in the recently published studies eligible for this updated review of US-licensed COVID-19 vaccines. Conclusions and Relevance: COVID-19 vaccines were consistently associated with a reduction in the risk of hospitalization and other outcomes, including among individuals at high risk of severe COVID-19, such as infants, and those who were pregnant. No new safety concerns were identified.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
